When bliss comes with recognition, life shines in all living and non-living things and spreads as the Paramatma in everything. The Guru is one who spreads like this.

[GV3:P1526] One does not necessarily have to ask to know this Guru. It will be experienced by itself through a gradually beginning realization, the passage of time, and through messages from other realized souls. That experience will become one's own. Asking and knowing is like a kind of sermon series. Asking and knowing should only be in the form of advice from the Guru. If it is through other means, there will be doubt. Doubt will lead to deficiency.

[GV3:P1527] Whatever one does in this world, it is all for the Guru. In a state of surrender, whatever is seen in God must be seen in the Guru. Then the disciple must realize that the Guru is the one who has seen, experienced, and realized everything from merging with the five elements to reaching bliss. Then God, Brahman, and Parabrahman are all one. It is the duty of the disciple to know that the Guru is that one. When one rises to the pinnacle of knowledge in birds, animals, and all living beings and becomes capable of knowing God, the disciple is the one who realizes that the Guru's life has functioned up to the knowledge that touched the five elements and reached the Anandamaya Kosha. At the same time, the one who attains liberation from all these living and non-living things, all elements, and all koshas and stands as knowledge itself should be addressed as the Guru. When a person approaching that Guru gets a small realization with the Guru as witness, his inner self will whisper that this Guru is the path to the ultimate goal. Coming to the certainty that what cannot be achieved no matter how much one acts and sacrifices will be achieved when compassion comes, the disciple should act keeping only that as the goal. Such a person can understand how the blissful realization born of compassion and love has functioned through the five senses and the five elements, grown, destroyed and liberated everything, and how this Guru has functioned in the beginning, end, and nature of that destruction. Then it will be clear what the Guru is, what the disciple is, why the Guru was made the medium, and what he will become. That is why one understands that matters to be asked and known should be from nearby and that if one is nearby, there is no need to ask anything.

[GV3:P1528] Sitting near or far is not the issue. Closeness is of the mind. If you sit near, you can hear the Guru's mind speaking. However, because the body has gone, there is a need to ask and know some things. When known with cause and effect, there is no need for questions and answers.

[GV3:P1529] The Guru's intention is to elevate the disciple to his own level. That is, the Guru and disciple become one. Nature is contained within the Guru and disciple. There is nothing left that is not contained within the Guru and disciple. Sometimes, it may even happen that the disciple is elevated slightly more than the Guru. Sri Ramakrishna and Vivekananda are an example of this. Sri Ramakrishna did not have literacy, worldly knowledge, or book knowledge. To him, a learned person came as a disciple. That is Vivekananda. There are many others like this.

[GV3:P1530] Usually, Guru lineages are developed through one path—devotion, knowledge, action, or yoga. If a boundary is not set for it, everything will become a mixed-up mess. Whichever path one grows through, the destination must be the same—when reaching perfection, just as many streams and rivers reach the ocean. A Guru lineage is one where one believes in the Guru's path, acts upon it, brings it into experience, and handles it and makes others handle it in a way that is equally useful to the land and the people. The goodness of the land can be seen leaping and growing towards it.

[GV3:P1531] However, our Guru lineage has an exalted uniqueness compared to all this. Here is a knowledge that unravels everything associated with a life, and whatever mistakes have occurred in action until today. When talking about what is related to life, asceticism is only a part of it. In the Chatur Yuga, which is divided into four ages, Kali Yuga is the age of purification. A disciple wealth that thinks about and retrieves the karma, the lifestyle of the great souls, their skill in action, and their hardships from the other three yugas has been gifted by God to this Guru lineage. This disciple wealth, which adds what has passed, what needs to be added to it, and if the glory of God decreases, adds that too and continues to work, is the grace of God upon this Guru lineage.

[GV3:P1532] Guruvani

[GV3:P1533] Santhigiri Janmadinapujitha Samarpana Pathippu - 2001

[GV3:P1534] How to achieve the merit of life

[GV3:P1535] There is a widespread belief that if a direct experience occurs, everything is achieved. There is also a thought that everything can be achieved through the application of siddhi (supernatural power). Because of this, the distinction between what is siddhi and what is knowledge has been lost. Siddhi had its influence during the time of the devas (gods). But now it is not the time of the devas. A direct experience is not going to achieve anything in this era. What takes many births of suffering to acquire can be achieved in one birth in Kali.

[GV3:P1536] Understand what we can do that is foundational and do that. Mere eloquence is useless. This involves great effort. This is an event that transforms man. That is, the course of action that has gone wrong over the ages and our own mistakes must be corrected. You have thought about why, even with so much experience here, there is so much struggle. All the vermin must come here and dissolve. Only by troubling us will it dissolve. Such difficulties do not exist in other places. In South India, there is no ashram discipline at all. In North India, there is some discipline. Kerala is a place where there is no such discipline at all. Because it was the Sudra who ruled here. Due to the dominance of the upper castes, knowledge could not be imparted to the Nairs and those below them. Among the ancient rulers, Kshatriyas were only in Kochi. The others were made up by Brahmins in disguise. Community feasting was only for the king. The members of the king's family had no entry. Such a rule existed in Travancore.

[GV3:P1537] People grew up like wild grass. People got some liberation only because Christians came here. India got independence because the Christians came. It's not that they didn't do some harm. They are experiencing its results today. They have ruled many places. But today, their name is not even in those arenas. Through them, many degradations have come upon us.

[GV3:P1538] What do we really need? While traveling through all these paths, one should cry and pray whatever one does, saying, 'We don't have the knowledge we should have, please give us all the knowledge we need.'

[GV3:P1539] I will talk about caste. If you talk like that, it will be a mistake. I am talking to eradicate caste. Anyone can understand that. Chandala and Brahmin, everyone is a member here. Everyone has the freedom to work here. During Narayana Guru's time, not a single Brahmin went there. It was due to the peculiarity of the royal rule at that time. Chithira Thirunal, desiring to have Narayana Guru serve food, sat in the Varkala temple and invited the Guru. The Guru said, 'We should not come there; you can come here and see me.' But coming to Sivagiri was difficult. So that did not happen. In a situation where even if a fly is allowed, an Ezhava should not be allowed, desiring to make the Guru do such an act is no small thing. But a ban came in such a way that it could not be done.

[GV3:P1540] How strongly the degradation spread by one group has taken root! It is in this place that we happened to be born and live. The one who has suffered all this will naturally develop a tendency to cut down the one who made him suffer. That should not happen here.

[GV3:P1541] Because the king was not a Kshatriya, we lost the knowledge to respect an ascetic. Because the king was obedient to the Vedic scholar. If the king is like this, how will the subjects be! We must be very careful because we have these shortcomings. The Vedic scholar knows rituals and rules. They might have behaved very trivially in front of us. But they would have done what is needed for their Acharya. If you don't have that knowledge, you will be pushed back into the old culture again.

[GV3:P1542] You have no sacrifice at all. It is said that Westerners have no sacrifice. But their diligence is not small. In places like China, women go to work on the seventh day after childbirth. Here, how long do you need four people to nurse them! From Ochira northwards, a pond is a furlong long. Seeing the rich people building stone steps to let Brahmin women bathe in a place where a handful of sand cannot be found, others also learned to do it. The children of one mother were divided into four as carpenter, brass-smith, goldsmith, and blacksmith, a thread was given on their left side, they were named Vishwakarma, and pooja was also started. In how many ways did women sitting at home become bad! Women became the worst among them. Eventually poverty struck, and beauty was lost. In this state, they were degraded. You must see this with pain. I am not talking about today's Vedic scholars. They are poor. It was their ancestors who did this. If they do not understand this, they will develop a violent nature.

[GV3:P1543] The Ezhavas have an arrogance. Only a learned person can tie a cloth on his head, drink toddy, and say 'I am somebody'. Some people converted and became Christians and Muslims. What the arrogant say to God is also arrogance. After the pooja and offerings, there is a saying—if what I think doesn't happen, I will throw you far away! The people in the forest come to the king with honey in a bamboo tube and give it turning their backs. They only call him 'king'. This is exactly the nature of the Ezhavas—arrogance.

[GV3:P1544] The Namboothiris of that time disciplined the Ezhavas with laws. Some studied Sanskrit and became Vaidyas (physicians). Everyone started respecting the Vaidyan. The Vaidyan studies very hard. They want to get ahead of the Vedic scholar—this is the desire. For that, they also learn a little sorcery. This Vaidyan comes and keeps others away. Ponamban, Thandan, Channan, Chovan, Panicker—these are all titles bought with money. A while ago, in Varanappally, I have seen Ezhava women being called 'Kochamma' by those belonging to their own community. They did even the worship of God without humility and became arrogant in knowledge and everything they touched. They never show humility. No matter how great a person they see, they will not bend their necks. Muslims will kneel even if they are on the road during prayer time. This is intended so that at least some people learn a custom. In the early days, Nataraja Guru said that even bowing down to Sree Narayana Guru was wrong. But after going to America and coming back, he scolded and made them bow down.

[GV3:P1545] Narayana Guru first taught them how to cook and eat. Then he taught them to offer sacrifices. Then temple etiquette. He taught them one by one. Finally, he offered himself for Guru Pooja, took a handful of the Guru Pooja rice, and gave the rest as Prasad. But still, the community did not understand. Some would say that Swami did not prescribe Pooja. Then why the temple? Wherever they go, they stand in arrogance.

[GV3:P1546] The society as a whole has such a shortcoming. We must understand this and act. Humility must be the greatest. Obedience must be greater than that. The greatest of all is worthiness.

[GV3:P1547] I have lived in Sivagiri as a young boy, picking flowers, and spending time in the cowshed and the dining hall. I grew up observing and learning all this. On many days, I have cried in pain. Only if there is love or obedience can they be reformed. How can we change all this? Those of you who live here must change this without eating or sleeping. When trying to uplift what has fallen, there will only be people to criticize. You must suppress and destroy your mischief and struggle. Otherwise, it will be like the penance done by Durvasa. He only had time to curse and do penance.

[GV3:P1548] People of all communities will come here. Everyone will feel that this is their central place. We are a small group. There are people of all types in this group. That is not our greatness, but God's grace. You must serve as if serving the Guru. Whatever you do, whatever you utter, consider it as serving the Guru and as the Guru's word. Whoever comes, they should not get the love they get from here from anywhere else. You must serve without looking at who they are. Some may think that they are being worshipped. But we must do what we have to do. If we do this, it will grow like a country without us knowing.

[GV3:P1549] Guruvani

[GV3:P1550] Santhigiri Janmadinapujitha Samarpana Pathippu - 2001

[GV3:P1551] The Lightness of the Guru and the Truth of the Disciple

[GV3:P1552] If the mother in a house is a bit clever, words that bring ruin to the house will surely fall. Father and mother equally wish that their children should be good. But at times, the father will say, 'They are your children.' Then none of his children are there! At times, the mother will also say, 'The bad deeds of your father.' There the father and children became separate. This is what we are familiar with in our homes. This is how disruption occurs in the house, knowingly or unknowingly. There are a few houses where, no matter how bad the father and mother are in any way, they live carefully, thinking that their own children should not become bad. That can be seen even in some poor people's homes. However, we do not usually see this culture in most of our homes. It means that the love in homes is based on a condition.

[GV3:P1553] The love of God is far beyond all this. The love of the Guru-disciple relationship is also beyond our thoughts. The concept of the Guru is not something that can be understood by thinking. What is called the love of God is the love of the Guru. The Guru will never speak highly of the disciple. He will only say that no one as bad as you has been born in the world. But it doesn't mean that he will become bad.

[GV3:P1554] The mother has one kind of love. The love of the father and the Guru is different. No matter how he is, for the mother, he is her child. It is not like that for the father. No matter how he is, he must be a good person. It is not enough that he is good to me. He must also be good to the world. This is the father's wish. The mother is not like that. She will bathe him, give him butter, give him ghee, and raise him. The father's words come from a mind as clear as the sky. The mother will speak words as hard as a mountain. Even if his whole life is eaten away, the mountain will still remain. That is also a knowledge.

[GV3:P1555] Mother, father, then Guru. The gods and ancestors who have come to the position of the Guru are not correct. The mistake that came from that is what the mother, father, and children suffer and endure. Those who have studied Sanskrit know the Guru and Kumara in Kumarasambhava. That Guru-disciple knowledge is knowledge according to the difference in state. An unfortunate situation where the model of this knowledge could not be taken occurred through the Trimurti system. In the Trimurti system, Shaktheyam is what stands strong. Shaktheyam means the mother's play. Whatever the mother shows is Shaktheyam. If the mother is Shaktheyam, the father must also be Shaktheyam. If the father does not become Shaktheyam, it will be wrong. No matter what kind of mother she is, she is still the mother to the children. No matter what kind of father he is, he is still the father. Someone who cannot be called a father will become Shaktheyam. If neither the mother nor the father is Shaktheyam, they will remain as Potti (a term used to refer to certain Brahmins). If it is Vaishnavam, both must be Vaishnavam. If one is a Vaishnavan and the other is not, they will become Potti.

[GV3:P1556] So the culture of all this is different. We should not see God like that. All this, everything seen here belongs to God. All this, starting from the five elements, belongs to God. What is seen, known, heard, and kept informative through the five senses is also knowledge. When it joins the Panchakoshas, it comes to the part of recognition. The Panchakoshas act into the life which is the centralization of the five elements. That life operates into the soul of the Supreme.

[GV3:P1557] The part of knowledge that reaches up to the five senses starting from the five elements, divided and taken, is what becomes the soul of the Supreme. Who becomes the soul of the Supreme? The one with the love that says 'his pain is my own pain'. This love cannot be understood if told in words. It is the glory seated deep within the mind. The aspect of the glory of that life cannot be easily discovered by anyone. Even if discovered, its measure cannot be found.

[GV3:P1558] The Guru is like a goldsmith who knows the standard of this love, the glory of life. If it is not a disciple with self-merit who is suited for such a Guru, they will quarrel and part ways. It is useless to say 'I am also a disciple'.

[GV3:P1559] We often wish that the Guru should see the greatness of our life. But the Guru does not say that. If someone comes, the Guru will understand what the deficiency in his life is. But he doesn't tell him 'you have this deficiency'. The Guru will change it for him only through his life. Otherwise, this guy will try to take everything and turn it upside down. With that, he will also be gone. His seat will also be gone. Like if you attach a good burner and put a small pot on it, the pot will shatter. Only a patient disciple can understand and take this. He is the one who earns. One who doesn't have it cannot earn. The disciple must be able to say that the Guru scolded him severely, didn't respect his words, made him look bad, didn't trust him, and said 'you are bad to the world'. The literature of today is what comes repeatedly pouring in after that side which gives inspiration to say this. How is the sun depicted in today's literature? 'It burst and fell' and so on. Just like if you scoop some cow dung and throw it up, if it is loose cow dung, it will scatter into small pieces. Giving an explanation after looking at its beauty is our literature today. The progressives are also those who enjoy the beauty of the explanation of this bursting and unfolding.

[GV3:P1560] This is not the explanation the Guru gives the disciple. The skill of the Guru is to bring the art system that reaches the theory of evolution of life and the humility system of knowledge included in it into Dharma, bring it to karma, and add it together. This cannot be obtained in a day. The Guru cannot be understood in a day.

[GV3:P1561] What is the Dharma of the disciple? No matter how long he stays with him or what he experiences, he will not have any desire of his own. If there is anything, it will be included in the Guru's will. His mind will not go to anything else. Even if one's father becomes very bad, will he cease to be the father?

[GV3:P1562] Haven't you heard of a poet named Prabhakara? Because Prabhakaran was a good disciple, the Guru scolded, beat, and punished him most severely. He would not punish any other children like that. Even if he beat them, he would give one or two blows. Not him, he would beat him right even if he writhed in pain, and only then move away. Therefore, he was sad. Seeing him always being beaten and scolded like this, the mother says, 'The Guru has no love for you, you go somewhere.' Who is this mother? The Guru's wife. This Guru's wife had often asked, 'Why are you always punishing him like this?' He wouldn't answer. Even though told to the mother and told by the mother, there was no rescue. Finally, he decided, 'Now I will just kill the Guru.' For that, he sat with a large stone kept on the loft. Let it be true or false. The matter is good for instantaneous imagination. When it was time, the Guru came. The mother seriously said again, 'Why are you punishing him like this? Isn't he the best disciple?' The Guru said, 'You won't understand this. He is the one growing well up to my intention. If he is a little more careful, he will become greater than me.' Hearing this, he threw the stone away, ran down, and fell there. 'I can suffer any punishment you want.' The story says he smoldered from the chaff. It does not mean he smoldered from the chaff and died, but it must be understood that he smoldered till his end. The other is a story told to be effective.

[GV3:P1563] The relationship between the disciple and the Guru cannot be justified with explanations. The love the Guru has for the disciple should not be revealed until the end. If revealed like that, he will become bad.

[GV3:P1564] Guruvani

[GV3:P1565] Santhigiri Janmadinapujitha Samarpana Pathippu - 2001

[GV3:P1566] Knowing where and how liberation and salvation are

[GV3:P1567] In the Dharma of Kali, we were the ones who should have been at the forefront. The rulers practiced and taught us not to come like that. Our parents, ancestors, and children are their groups. What can such people like us impart to this world?

[GV3:P1568] I am introducing a familiarity to you to escape from this profound danger. I am also someone who grew up from a tradition like yours. When I had an experience, some who stood with me got angry and moved away from me. When asked why they got angry, they cried in pain. What is this peculiar mind? Those who came together with me to share goodness with the world stand aside and cry, unable to do anything. Even if there is pain, they must go away from me. They definitely know that their stand is not right. But they cannot hold on. They will not act, and they will mislead the one who acts and make him walk away.

[GV3:P1569] This tree has broken the handle of the axe of all karmas. Our ancestors are the authors of this shattered familiarity with karma. Should we change from this or not? There are some good people who unwittingly fell into this karma. They are the authors of the Puranas, religious scholars, and their propagators. It is impossible to resolve this matter that brought them down and those who fell through them.

[GV3:P1570] Before Sri Krishna was born, Kamsa received an ethereal voice that Krishna would be born and that he would destroy him if he did not behave properly. We have been taught that Kamsa was wicked. Kamsa is not wicked. Kamsa was the one who showed the courage to believe in the words of the devas and devis who appeared, make others believe, rule, and make others rule. But how did his end become? He was possessed by Kali (anger/madness). In that Kali, he forgot the truth. Because he played away from the path prompted by the devas and devis who stood hidden, he did not know the weight and greatness of the ethereal voice and the divine story behind it. Kamsa is not wicked, base, or contemptible. The same karmic dosha that describes the demonic nature of Ravana's ten heads is also imparted through the description of Kamsa.

[GV3:P1571] It cannot be said that all this is completely a fabricated story. Why? Let's take the familiarity of our own little Kerala itself. Where do they mostly go to call upon God? Sabarimala. How? They go to call upon God completely drunk, to the extent of vomiting their guts out. As soon as you go like that, you can see the Lord, and you will get salvation too. Then what is the difficulty? What an easy time and environment we are born in to get salvation.

[GV3:P1572] What use is the knowledge imparted to us by this group that imparted ignorance in this state? In the past, Brahmins did not go to Sabarimala. In those days, the Malarayans (a tribal community) were the ones doing the pooja there. They are the Kochu Kadutha and Valiya Kadutha and so on. Today, by touching the feet of eminent ministers, the chief Thanthris (head priests) are taking and serving morsels of knowledge for us. If you give ten or fifteen lakhs, they will serve a little, otherwise, they will not serve at all. It is because of the Vedic dharma and the pride of scholarship served in this state that we are ruined.

[GV3:P1573] Now, some people from Palakkad, Kozhikode, and Kodungallur, who are very sincerely ruling the hills, are coming here. They cannot stand on the hill now. Do you know why? All these ignorant people climb up the hill, push to the point of bending the iron rails placed as barricades, and if someone dies in it, they pick them up and throw them in the forest. It rots there, bugs and flies swarm from there, and with the stench too, these wild monkeys cannot sit there and are running hither and thither. Therefore, those working there cannot stand there.

[GV3:P1574] This is the course of karma that has been passed down. If we assume that our karma will be fulfilled by such paths of devotion, what can be done? Showing the worst skill in this caste, that is what is passed on to our children. I am telling you a medium for this.

[GV3:P1575] Looking at the circumstances in which I was born and brought up, I would never tell you a part of knowledge. I didn't have such knowledge. Then all I know is only love. When that love came, and I handled people with that love, people have exploited that love. Because I grew up behind this danger, I did not study any of the knowledge served by scholars. That is, I did not study the 'Prathyakshavaham Dharmam' in the Trimurti system or in the reasoning from religious consciousness in the prophetic system. I have only looked into the visions of Sree Narayana Guru, Sree Sankara, and other such experientialists without scholarship, which they wrote down.

[GV3:P1576] Understand the truth that none of the heroes and warriors who have rolled and fallen through the old and new beliefs imposed on us in Hinduism and prophetic religions have helped complete this karma, nor will they help. These scholars have created a karma that is of no use to us in another way too. That is the biographies of great souls. In the biographies of Sri Krishna, Sri Rama, Ramathirtha, Ramana Maharshi, etc., the course of action of similar miraculous deeds has been passed down.

[GV3:P1577] There are some other shortcomings in us too. In my younger days, I have had occasion to go to places with some concepts of devas and devis and places where demons jump. There, they will give it to the demon. What is this demon? I have felt fear in these places. When they asked to give it to the demon, I would stand and look at it. There is a stone buried there. They take it near that stone and do some rituals. I didn't understand anything. What is installed on that stone are the Manthramoorthys (deities of mantras), Yanthramoorthys (deities of yantras), Thanthramoorthys (deities of tantras), and then Brahmarakshass. Brahmarakshass is a karma that tells and shows us about the protection of Brahman. In the language of the Prophet, this is Jinn. In the language of Jesus, it is called a demon. Thus, saying it in each one's language, they have kept all these karmas without letting them go. These karmas have not left any of these great men alone. Jesus asks, 'My God, why have you forsaken me.' The Prophet also said the same thing. This demon itself has guided Sri Sankara to jump into the gorge and guided many great men who showed siddhis to their end. Sree Narayana Guru has given a description keeping that in mind:

[GV3:P1578] Just as nature catches and whirls,

[GV3:P1579] Even the virtuous, alas, are whirled.

[GV3:P1580] What I have to say is that we must work to leave this mischief. This mischief in nature has caught and whirled us. It is the great men that it has whirled. It is difficult to escape from this endlessly entangled karma.

[GV3:P1581] For that, I will speak up to the experience I have got. You can believe it, love it, take it, know it, or discard it; you can do whatever you want according to your ability.

[GV3:P1582] There are many works for which I have struggled a lot. With the effort of twenty-nine years, after reaching the state of 'Prathyakshavaham Dharmam', finally it became impossible to take it back. In the twenty-ninth year, I took it back. 'Father, why have you forsaken me? What is this, Father, that you have shown me, who came believing that there is nothing else but you?' - God helped me in the twenty-ninth year to take back the karma that made Jesus and the Prophet speak in sorrow like this, and the karma that led many great souls to a tragic end.

[GV3:P1583] Are these great men bad? All of you who know through love know that none of them are bad. Those who stand with mischief might not have been able to know. It is a necessary sense of duty for my contemporaries, scholars, or laymen to inquire what mistake happened to all these great men.

[GV3:P1584] Demons, devas, devis, rishis, sanyasis, siddhas, etc., do exist. If they didn't exist, they wouldn't have a name. But instead of going after everything that exists, calling its name, knocking on its door, and swimming to catch it, it is much better to just sit quietly.

[GV3:P1585] I am telling a story of my experience. In the manner of 'Prathyakshavaham Dharmam', all devas and devis up to the Trimurti state came. I wrote down quite a number of them. We have heard that there are thirty-three crore devas, right? It is neither thirty-three crore nor three hundred crore. I wrote down three hundred and fifty crore and stopped. I also happened to see all these three hundred and fifty crore. All this does exist.

[GV3:P1586] In what spheres do all these stand? Scholars call these 'Bhoomikas' (realms/stages). They don't say this after knowing it. They say what they read from someone else. The very place we stand is one 'Bhoomi' (earth/realm). The karma that can travel in seven such realms is what is being said. What is beyond this has no form. How will we find this formless karma? How will we find it and open it?

[GV3:P1587] I went up to the Trimurti and opened it and saw. Where can one get the eye to go beyond that and see? It can only be seen with the eye given by God. When talking about Krishna and Arjuna, we understand that Arjuna managed with the eye given by Krishna. Because Arjuna chose Krishna as his Guru, Arjuna could stand on the path shown by Krishna and do the work. One might ask, couldn't Krishna have done that work? Krishna did some work that only Krishna could do. Why didn't he do other work? Because there was no one among us to say this, we are caught in a karmic dosha of listening to the karma of saints whose deeds were seen, heard, enjoyed, and praised, and believing in it as it flows down. If we are to be able to change from this karmic dosha, moving away from these matters filled with the Trimurti system, Devendra system, and Nakshatra system, what should we do instead? Where and when should we move? When will we walk and all these matters reach us along with us? We need a way to know all this. That eye is for knowing that. I believe that eye is the truth. It is in practice here now.

[GV3:P1588] I am talking about this eye. A poor poet said, cross the six steps and go there, and Shiva can be seen. When he says cross the six steps and go there, and Shiva can be seen, he believes that all the walking up to there is complete. In a way, that is also true. This has two meanings. One, Shiva takes form and stands up to there. Another, this viewer has reached a level without form. Similarly, this eye and this karma must be one that goes beyond the Trimurti system where the Vaishnavan, Shaivan, and Shaktheyan stand formless, and the Nakshatra system that stands beyond it, and knocks, opens, and sees.

[GV3:P1589] This karma is what a person must complete in his life's duty. What all will be contained in the life story of such people? History says about them: 'He did penance for ten thousand years.' That means he took many births. We usually don't think like this. It is the total account of the penance done throughout those births that is stated. This is written down what he saw and acquired.

[GV3:P1590] Similarly, if our life story is revealed through us itself, how we lived, how much we lived deviating from the truth, how much we lived in harmony with the truth - that life should be one that sees this. This is a vision. Those who experienced this vision have written it down. I mentioned earlier, that is Sree Sankara and Sree Narayana Guru and others. The Veda originated from the transcendental peak. That means, light came into being, knowledge came into being.

[GV3:P1591] We can say whatever we want in this according to our knowledge. What will we get if we explain its meaning? Does it mean that we have borne it or that humility is brimming in it? We should fully take it like this. Take this vision up to our knowledge. The mischiefs of scholars and laymen are not only unnecessary for it, but they are also needless.

[GV3:P1592] Suppose we see a thing that we have never seen or heard of before. What name will we give it? Whatever it may be, we don't know. Similarly, because we are not familiar with it, whether it is bitter or sweet, black or white, don't we just say we can't do it and back away from it?

[GV3:P1593] What is in the karmic part performed as deva, devi, rishi, sanyasi, jnani, and bhakta? What authority do we have in this karma? How can we accomplish it? Our mistake lies in where we should have thought like this. This mistake happened to Arjuna too. That is why Krishna gave him his eye and made him do it. Yet Arjuna cried aloud. In that place, how can we take Arjuna as a model? The mistake happened to Arjuna, the first disciple of Sri Krishna, a scholar, an ascetic, and a great archer. This is where we should think, how thick this mistake is.

[GV3:P1594] We see Sri Krishna in a great position as God. Scholars say he burned in the fire of yoga (yogagni). Okay, he burned in yogagni. What can we do? When it is said he burned in yogagni, does it mean we should blindly believe it? Let it burn or perish in whatever fire. But a scene that we haven't grasped yet is growing fully. What is it? Krishna ran around all parts of India, stood on another shore, and built Dwaraka on an island there. It was when Dwaraka was completed that an arrow hit Krishna's foot. It is said that the arrow hit because of the hunter's mistake. Whatever mistake it may be, Sri Krishna takes back the words he had spoken until then: 'Hey, at least take these women and children and get them safely to a shore.' But the great archer Arjuna couldn't save even a child. These wild men, or rogues, beat and destroyed them all. Arjuna reaches a state where even his bow is broken, and he cries aloud. If this helplessness came to this Arjuna, through what will we advance?

[GV3:P1595] Now, this Dwaraka has sunk. How will we catch what has sunk? But we will not think about any of this. We are swallowing what the scholars say as it is. We are the root of an ignorance that swallows thoughtlessly in this state.

[GV3:P1596] If we come to Kerala, we have 'Ammachis' (mother goddesses) here. Kodungallur Amma, Cherthala Karthyayani Amma. All this has become 'Amma'. Along with that, how many other mothers. Thus, this whole land is sitting as 'Amma'. To Bhadrakali, how many hands have been scooped and given. God has no other job. Similarly, showing the form of Ghatotkacha scooping up snakes, we look at it and enjoy it. What can we do with our own karma when we stand confused, believing in the works of scholars and laymen? What is in the hands of this artist who shows Bhadrakali strung with so many hands, legs, and skulls?

[GV3:P1597] Here we become empty. What is Bhadrakali? There is a good meaning in that word. 'The one who burns for safety' (Bhadram Kalunnaval). But we have not taken it in that sense. When we get angry, we feel like calling the girls we raise, 'She is a Bhadrakali indeed'. This is the knowledge we have bitten and swallowed.

[GV3:P1598] What do we know, hiding knowledge behind the ignorance of showing off in this state? In this state, Bhadra does not have the decoration of many hands, legs, or skulls. It doesn't stand for anyone's worship either. This doesn't babble anything for anyone's happiness. But this won't be explained to anyone.

[GV3:P1599] Bhadra is about a life force that goes away completely showing its karma in accordance with the times. Other than that, Bhadra doesn't have the hands, heads, and tongues mentioned in songs of praise. Because we don't understand this, it is our ignorance that we draw many such pictures, worship them, and see Bhadra behind it. If we see the real Bhadra, we won't be able to recognize it. I said earlier that it has no decorations. It is seen with and without clothes. We must see what this absence indicates.

[GV3:P1600] The messenger for that—Avadhoot. The woman brimming with compassion of the gnana character who walks as a messenger for that is Bhadra. Keeping the principle of the soul completely safe, it is one that walks without thinking whether it has clothes, ornaments, special intelligence, or decorations. This doesn't come in the nature of science. We won't get it to worship or praise. Once it does its karma, it will be gone. Instead, ours is the ignorance that remains confined to the karma of mocking by reciting a kind of Vedantic verses while bathing one with many hands and legs scooped and attached.

[GV3:P1601] We have some difficulties in practicing *Avadhutha* in this way. We have been taught about it in a different way. See with our own eyes what a God, Goddess, sage, ascetic, wise man, and devotee are. It cannot be achieved just by writing, drawing, decorating, and explaining meanings. Ra-mayanam, if you just say mayanam, what is mayanam? To read it together, 'Ra' is also needed. Therefore, 'Ra' must be set aside. Such combined reading and interpretation is the absurdity standing behind the nihilism of scholars.

[GV3:P1602] This 'Ra' will not simply change like that; that is not the way to get rid of ego. We should not learn by hanging onto the ignorance of such Vedantic verses. This is not what you need. If you want to live, you must eat. Even for eating, an ego is needed. For that, action (karma) is needed. Where there is karma, there is ego. You have the intelligence, capability, and capacity to think about what you need.

[GV3:P1603] If we hold onto our Guru, this God, Goddess, sage, ascetic, prophet, and wise man should all come before us. This mentioned Bhadra should also come. This is the aspect. In the karma that you and your ancestors have been holding onto and worshipping, all these mentioned entities are present. Even Durga is there. Only this Bhadra is not in it. You might strongly oppose this karma by standing in the karma that is absorbed in the nihilism shown by jumping, dancing, and gaining strength before you. Alright, so be it, you can keep and worship all these. But you must hold onto the Guru and see this karma. It has a specific aspect. By liberating the ancestors and Yogis who are trapped in all the karmas you have worshipped to date, that karma is being revealed to you. At its physical level, in its heart, or in its broad vision, whatever realms there are, the heart-touching form of the Guru stands in all of them. In that light, you are seeing all these things. This is that aspect. There are no other justifications for it.

[GV3:P1604] This is the karma called direct perception. As long as all these do not come and become submissive to our Guru, there is never any liberation for them from it. That is why it is said that the Guru is Brahma, Vishnu, and Shiva. Nothing happens just by seeing this. The purity of karma to know about the changing states of all these must also be transferred to us. The person who transfers it is the core of our doctrine. This is not a scientific formula. It is the pure wisdom that imparts the karma that can be acquired by everyone, regardless of whether they are male or female. You are observing whether the soul form of the Guru exists in the inner heart of all of you—father, mother, children, husband, wife, and all the past Mahatmas.

[GV3:P1605] There are many magical formulas stating that the dead go and stand there, go and stand here, merge into the wind and air, and then stand nowhere. I am not saying any of this. You can see this. You can see it truly. This is my experience. With this experience, there is no need to guide you by offering coconuts and mangoes from here and there. None of those are things that help in self-purification.

[GV3:P1606] The dharma of Kali is the dharma we have received to become a human, a God, a sage, an ascetic, a wise man, and a devotee. Each of you is a scientific formula that transforms it and makes it completely holy. Your offspring should be children who guide humans to the karma of seeking the path to liberation. This can only be imbibed from your home and not from an ascetic monastery. If you have such children in your home, there is no doubt that our lineage will reach as perfect holy souls who have found such a position, sanctified you in that karma, raised you completely, and followed dharma. This is my experience.

[GV3:P1607] Guruvani

[GV3:P1608] Santhigiri Janmadinapoojitha Samarppanappathippu - 2001

[GV3:P1609] The Correction and Dharma of the Visionary Aspect

[GV3:P1610] Those with vision will understand, if they pay attention, that the light they see is different from the light of Gods and Goddesses. Different kinds of light—those who see it may not possess the wisdom to recognize it. That should be asked and learned from the Guru. Only the Guru can recognize it.

[GV3:P1611] After the creation, a very long time passed without any evolution, without even knowing that the earth existed. It took ages even to shape the divine will. Devaloka and Brahmaloka have their own truthfulness. If man has a truthfulness, he can see the goodness of God. The earth where that good man lives will change because of his goodness. A good man means one who has virtuous qualities embedded in his life.

[GV3:P1612] A great soul stands in the atmosphere constantly performing penance. At the right time, that great soul is born on earth. Along with him, some unrighteous people are also born. Both groups argue that theirs is the right path.

[GV3:P1613] The glory of creation is that God's greatness is mixed in all living beings. Even a great soul may not be able to think and understand all this entirely. But it can be understood through dedication. It is believing and surrendering by seeing one as everything. In that faith, whether one beats, kills, or offers flowers and worships someone, it is not evil. But in the concept of Gods, this is not good. Even if he sees an evil, he will surrender: You are the cause of everything, you yourself change this, I am nobody. Just as mud becomes rock, his mind will become firm with this concept.

[GV3:P1614] Do not think that everything is possible as soon as you have a vision. One person saw a light, another got light by removing the veil, one saw Kali, another saw Shiva. He wrote and drew all this. It is the forms, pooja methods, and concepts created long ago that appear in visions over time. A meditating sage will peacefully observe even an ant moving. Then he will try to find the reason for it. And he will record it. This is the work of an ascetic. It is the design of these discoveries that we see later.

[GV3:P1615] After the Manu lineage went wrong, three Chatur Yugas lay empty. In the fourth Chatur Yuga, the Trimurti system was brought in through a concept. But it only experienced pleasure in Devaloka and did not progress further. The one who worshipped the sun said that is the greatest. The one who worshipped the moon said that is the greatest. The one who worshipped the earth said that is the greatest.

[GV3:P1616] The mistake happened through the great soul with vision in the Manu lineage. You made a second creation and it went wrong. We were also brought through that mistake. There must always be a prayer: Please bless us by giving us whatever is needed to join You without putting us in that mistake.

[GV3:P1617] How many great souls lived in recent times! How many of their followers were beaten to death! How much they suffered! It is in this way that each thing has been brought here. There must have been a lot of effort over a long time just to discover a medicine. How many people must have suffered. How many people must have sacrificed themselves for each great soul. How many had to become martyrs. Today everything is ripe for us. God has given us intelligence and ability. We must have a desire that no matter how much we work, it is not enough. We are the ones who must put it into practice. Just that the action should not go wrong.

[GV3:P1618] We need a holy war itself. We must be prepared by tying our words, mind, and actions behind one goal. Even though Jesus's followers were ignorant, they won the kingdom through dedication. That is the result of twenty-one years of crusades. A sacrificing person will never turn back from dedicated karma. When Vivekananda was in America, some locals came and abused Indian ascetics. Eventually, they abused his Guru too. Then he clenched his fist saying he would kill them if they spoke anymore. This is dedication. If it were you, you would just cover your head and walk away. Another example—Sri Ramakrishna's twelve inner disciples wandered without clothes, without food, without a place to keep the Guru's mortal remains, and finally stayed in a snake-infested forest. Vivekananda wandered here and there, sent them whatever money he could get, and fed them. You can do one thing: pray that these great souls who lived and worked with all their abilities in the past may be born as children in the wombs of the ignorant women of this lineage.

[GV3:P1619] We were born and grew up. And we will die. In between, there are only two things we face—good and bad. We should at least try to reduce one bad thing every day and bring in one good thing instead. If we raise our children properly, at least fifty out of a hundred will turn to work in God's path for us. We must remember that these children are the torches for tomorrow. Through intention, the dust of illusion standing in this atmosphere must be removed and the dust of goodness must be infused. You must take a careful pledge for this.

[GV3:P1620] Life within this faith is very great. You should work forgetting caste and religion. Otherwise, mistakes will happen. Strength is needed to work like this. Life will not be destroyed just because of a disease or lack of money.

[GV3:P1621] If you think my beauty is gone, my money is gone, then it is gone. You must have courage. If we look at the situation in Kerala from the time of the Ettuveetil Pillamars to the time of the British, we have a lot to do. One of us must do the service of a thousand people. We have a field with that much work. A thousand years of work must be successfully completed in one year. We must understand this magnitude and do it. The thoughts, intellect, and education of poor people like you will only go up to the level of reading and writing in a university. If we think about our parents and ancestors, we will understand that this is not enough. We have to rise so much higher. But it must be with humility.

[GV3:P1622] We must be careful that the action we do does not cause trouble to another. And it must be done honestly. If girls are comfortable at home, they won't do anything. How many people are needed just to help untangle her hair? The mother will also support that. When she becomes a mother, someone is needed to carry the child. No matter how many servants are hired, the matter will not be resolved. And the family will justify it grandly.

[GV3:P1623] Whatever thought emanates from us, that thought will cover us. Be careful of doing everything secretly. If you hide it, it won't be hidden. Nature will capture it immediately. Because of some actions of mothers in big houses, their children will suffer that deficiency. The children will be overly intelligent. By using this intelligence, their karma will go wrong. Except for all this, one who acts according to what he sees can achieve everything. If the work done from the ashram is to become a sacrifice, it must be done with all humility. One must accept everyone's scolding. Even those without any business might scold. They should say it. We must listen and tolerate it. Then all our sinful powers will drain away.

[GV3:P1624] Purity must be brought into karma. We should try to earn merit by doing that karma regardless of what karma is done and where it is done. No matter for whom the karma is or what karma it is, it must be done purely. One should not have happiness towards those who speak well and anger towards those who speak badly. When Sattva grows, a curiosity will arise. From that, emotion will arise. We must ensure that it does not harm us. One must have a sense of purpose about why he came. We must work in a way that our hearts melt. Then through Guru Seva, you will attain greatness. You must live decently, be careful, and serve. Obstinacy and vengeance should be for bringing in the part towards goodness. If you do these things carefully, you will definitely get the wages for your work.

[GV3:P1625] Guruvani

[GV3:P1626] Santhigiri Janmadinapoojitha Samarppanappathippu - 2001

[GV3:P1627] Some Historical Truths That Correct the Visionary Aspect

[GV3:P1628] I first came to Thiruvananthapuram accidentally. On the way from Aluva to Varkala. Back then, there was a boat from Aluva and Ernakulam to Alappuzha. And from Alappuzha to Kollam. Traveling like that, I reached Kollam. Then a friend said, since you are coming to South Travancore for the first time, let's go to the Sree Padmanabhaswamy Temple in Thiruvananthapuram.

[GV3:P1629] I had set out on the journey to go to Varkala. I came straight to Thiruvananthapuram. Even I don't know the reason for that. That was the time when royal rule existed.

[GV3:P1630] Standing in front of the temple and looking up, I saw a Sudarshana Chakra. It was also inside the temple back then. I don't know if it is there now. That is, after passing the bell tower, when climbing up and down the steps to enter the temple, there is a hall. A Sudarshana Chakra is drawn on it. Seeing this, I trembled. Because I have seen this before. Just that I was not sure of the place. When I saw this, it felt like reviving the memory of a contemporary event. Reviving the memory of a past-present-future (Trikala) event!

[GV3:P1631] At that time, only the building where the court functions was there. That building was constructed during the time of Moolam Thirunal. There are houses on all four sides of it. However, the buildings seen around it today were not there back then. Today, many buildings have come up in that compound. None of that was there then. But I had seen this place along with the buildings seen today (in a vision). I had seen this while in Aluva. I am coming here fifteen years after seeing it. That is why I was shocked when I saw it.

[GV3:P1632] Seeing this, I felt that there is some other place here. So I went inside the temple. That was the day the King (Chithira Thirunal) was coming there. I didn't know anything like you shouldn't stand there when the King comes. The Ananthashayanam idol is made of Salagramam. Nobody goes and stands in front of it. I didn't know this either. I was standing near a pillar. When the King came there, he looked at me carefully and left.

[GV3:P1633] I was quite fair-skinned back then. So they must have thought I was some Brahmin boy. Nobody else would stand there. There was nobody around me. When I looked, some people were standing far away, with cloth tied around their waists. They were worshipping the idol standing very far away. Even though I stood this close, neither the King nor his attendants said anything to me. These are the memories of my first trip to Thiruvananthapuram.

[GV3:P1634] From that day onwards, I had a thought that there is some place here—a place related to me. I walked from there. Walking continuously, I covered all four sides of the outer temple (Chuttambalam). From there, I walked east through the northern entrance. When I went down and looked there, something like a statue was sitting there. It wasn't covered back then. It was lying open. Seeing this, I remembered having seen it before. That is a Samadhi of mine. That place has not caught the attention of many. Even the people in the palace don't know about it.

[GV3:P1635] The name Thiruvananthapuram came from the presence of Ananthankadu there. This Samadhi is in this Ananthankadu. In that birth, my name was Anandan. This Samadhi dates back between a thousand and two thousand years. There is a concept in that Samadhi. What is that concept? I will talk about it later.

[GV3:P1636] There is a Cheraman Perumal Fort in Thalassery. This fort is located at the place ruled by Cheraman Perumal. When I visited there before, it was written "Cheraman Perumal Fort" on a stone in front of the college. I don't know if it is there today. This fort and Pulayanarkotta were connected. That is, the Pulayas had administrative power all over Kerala. Except that we know this from history, how can we identify that place today? Similarly, Oruvatilkotta. From there to this Ananthankadu, there was only a single path.

[GV3:P1637] It is said that I came and sat in Ananthankadu in Thiruvananthapuram. I was brought and seated there by the rulers of that time. I attained Samadhi sitting there. The concept held while sitting there is what I mentioned earlier. The Shudra must get power. When that concept succeeded and the Shudra was about to get power, the Ettuveetil Pillamars divided it entirely among themselves. Marthanda Varma's house got half a share. That is the Padmanabhapuram Fort. Padmanabhapuram Fort came into existence after power fell into the hands of the Ettuveetil Pillamars. God's ancient concept was the foundation for this rule and all these events to unfold.

[GV3:P1638] If you look at the Sree Ranganathaswamy Temple in Tamil Nadu, you can see that there is no other such installation anywhere else in India. The Ananthashayanam idol made of Salagramam. The installation in the Padmanabhaswamy Temple was made looking at this installation. It didn't match up to that extent here. The calculation stopped when it reached three enclosures here. The Sree Ranganathaswamy Temple has seven enclosures.

[GV3:P1639] I was talking about the concept that the Shudra should get power. What is the reason to conceive that the Shudra should get power? Because the era (Yuga) changed. Sri Krishna was the first person to come when the Yuga changed. Krishna was born in the Shudra clan. Because he was born in the Shudra clan, these kings chased him relentlessly everywhere in India. After that, he went and sat in Kubera's kingdom. Sitting there, he built Dwaraka. Before Dwaraka was completed, the sea took it away.

[GV3:P1640] The concept of Brahman that the Shudra should get power dates back many Kali Yugas ago! Brahmin, Kshatriya, Vaishya, Shudra. It is the concept of Brahman that this Shudra should become the ruler in the fourth phase, the Kali Yuga. This is the order of Yuga Dharma. Why did this order fail to materialize? I will talk about it later.

[GV3:P1641] Brahmins migrated here from East Asia. Most of them migrated to North India. They first came to the banks of the Saraswati River. Those who are known today as Konkanis (Gaud Saraswat Brahmins) lived there then. The immigrants chased them away and took possession. Thus, these early Brahmins fled to Goa. When immigration happened there too, they fled from there and scattered in many directions. Among those who ran, they returned here again. Having run all over this country, they lost a permanent place or language. The language they have accepted is Tulu. And it has no script. Furthermore, India lost its overall unification.

[GV3:P1642] What is the reason for India to end up in such a state? The error in the Manvantara calculation. That is, the calculation in the Manu system was lost in the later Trimurti system. In the Trimurti system, apart from mentioning Manvantara, it is not stated anywhere where, how, when, or why. What is currently ongoing is the seventh Manvantara. In this Manvantara, the Trimurti system has been running for twenty-five Chatur Yugas. The current Chatur Yuga is the twenty-eighth. Only the first three Chatur Yugas functioned correctly in this Manvantara. The subsequent three Chatur Yugas lay empty. Starting from the seventh Chatur Yuga, the Trimurti system was completed in ten Chatur Yugas. This Trimurti system ends by keeping everything up to that point in the form-name concept. Because of that, we became unable to know anything beyond the Devas. And there was no one left to teach us anything beyond this. During the Vedic period, Shudras had no authority, right?

[GV3:P1643] The concept of Brahman is a concept much older than the Trimurti system. This is the concept I hinted at earlier. When power came in this Kali Yuga, it fell into the hands of the Ettuveetil Pillamars. Among the shares divided and ruled by the eight families, the Marthandam Palace also got half a share. It is the heir to that half-share who finally captured it. That lies up to Edava. Travancore only extends up to there. The rest belongs to the King of Kollam, the King of Kottarakkara, the King of Mavelikkara, and beyond that, Ambalappuzha. The Cherthala taluk was given away during the time of Sakthan Thampuran.

[GV3:P1644] All this came about because of the concept in this Samadhi. This Samadhi is still there today in a recognizable state—a place where ants do not crawl. Where is a place where ants don't swarm if butter is kept? But if butter is kept in this Samadhi, ants won't touch it. Why is this history being told like this?

[GV3:P1645] The Saptarishis are the concept of some great souls from ancient times. Seven realms took shape from the concept of the Saptarishis. Two realms have no form. Both of them stand like huge clouds of smoke. The reason it transformed into a cloud of smoke is because this dharma order was broken.

[GV3:P1646] It is in the Kali Yuga, the fourth quarter, that the Shudra must obtain and fulfill power. If power is obtained, it won't be fulfilled just like that. For that, you must know about the concept of the Saptarishis from the time of creation. Knowing about this concept, they prevented the Shudra from gaining Vedic authority and scientific knowledge. At the same time, their history should not be lost either. The upper-caste classes acted intelligently for this.

[GV3:P1647] Three or four generations ago, there was only one class called Illathu Pillai among the Nairs. They were tall and physically strong. To degrade them, sixty-four divisions of Nairs were created. And they made all of them aggressors. Marthanda Varma's government gave participation to everyone. But these Illathu Pillais or these sixty-four Nair divisions didn't understand why this power came into their hands. Because the Shudra got administrative power without Vedic authority. A Shudra only knows how to serve. He doesn't know how to rule. You haven't understood this.

[GV3:P1648] The Namboothiri usurped the position of Cheraman Perumal. From the hands of the Namboothiri, it went into the hands of the Mughals. Even though history lies true like this, there is no way to know the reason why the dharma of Kali turned out this way. And there is no knowledge on how to uphold the truth that Kali belongs to the Shudra.

[GV3:P1649] Guruvani

[GV3:P1650] Santhigiri Janmadinapoojitha Samarppanappathippu - 2001

[GV3:P1651] Proof of the Visionary Aspect

[GV3:P1652] Our belief is that if we worship the Goddess, we will reach God. We adopt these worship practices considering them as mediums to God. We will not reach God. We will only end up in the realm of whatever we worship. We won't go beyond that. This has happened even to very great Mahatmas. How do we correct this? If this is the truth of the matter, shouldn't we seek the path to see God? We must have a determination for that.

[GV3:P1653] Even if it is a ghost, if we just try to remove it, it will not go away. Saying this is a ghost, this is useless, this is not needed, let it go, won't make it leave. That ghost will stay with you. Whatever the path may be—be it the Guru's, the prophet's, the Hindus', or Manu's—the matter is the same for everything. We must give liberation to everything we have worshipped. After granting liberation, we will let it go on whichever path it takes. When Gods, Goddesses, ascetics, and sages all stand in our lineage, the practice here is to surpass all this and see the truth. You might think this is a new topic. But it is not new, it is very old. This is a topic that has been forgotten from our memory.

[GV3:P1654] Through Guru pooja, liberation is granted to the worshipped entities. Once that is done, the people of that house should absorb its goodness. After performing Guru pooja, Venkatachalapathy, Jesus, Nabi, Buddha, Sankaracharya—all of them appear in visions. Everyone who comes and stays in the ashram has come like that. There are such men and women here. This Mexican guy, and many from various countries, came after realizing this. There is no caste, religion, or race for that. When this Mexican guy met me for the first time, he asked, "Do you know me?" And then he hugged my feet. How could he come here with such familiarity? This is a guidance that has reached his soul. This does not belong to anyone else. If I say that the Christian who comes here gets the Sri Sankara lineage, the Madhwa lineage, and then Jesus along with its lineage, how can you understand that? And they themselves are explaining what this Guru is. Otherwise, I shouldn't be saying that.

[GV3:P1655] This is not something inaccessible to others. Anyone in the world can get this. Women, men, everyone. It means that women should attain a masculine strength (spiritual prowess). Life energy is the same for both men and women, isn't it? There is no difference in that. The gross material elements must be shed. How? It can be broken through gross (Sthula), subtle (Sukshma), causal (Karana), and effect (Karya) states. That is, it can be seen grossly in everything. It can be seen in the subtle as well. The reason for that will be shown. This familiarity leads to finding God. They are seeing what they have been repeating over many births.

[GV3:P1656] One religion will say there is no rebirth. Another religion will say there is rebirth. Is it correct to say there is no rebirth while preaching the prophetic path? To preach the prophetic path, there must definitely be rebirth. It is said that this is the nth Nabi, right? Then there is rebirth. One has come as a continuation of what someone else established. They are the ones who come to renew something in each era. We cannot move forward by rejecting them. We cannot progress without accepting all of them, encompassing them, and giving them what needs to be given. Otherwise, we won't tell anyone to change their religion or caste.

[GV3:P1657] All the young children standing here are ones who see visions. Acharyas like Jesus embrace them and say, "Children, do not let this go wrong." What can we say to this? We should approach this only after understanding its nature at least a little bit.

[GV3:P1658] Let me tell you another experience. There are two churches after crossing the Thalassery bridge. One of them is St. Mary's Church. Nobody knew me outside back then. It was a time when I sat in a hut in Varkala Santhigiri. Someone called me three times. It was his younger brother's wedding. They are only two children. The father and mother are strong believers of St. Mary's Church. They go to St. Mary's Church every day. He came to me directed by someone. Then he told me, you must come there once. I asked why I should come. But he insisted. So the next morning, he took me in a car to Thalassery.

[GV3:P1659] When I went there, this father and mother had gone to church. It was Palm Sunday that day. After a while, they returned from the church. The younger brother of the person who brought me is a film producer in England. When it's time for him to get married, some illness strikes him. During the engagement, he either collapses there. Or he gets a boil in his armpit. Then he can't lower his arm. Consequently, the bride's family refuses. His marriage got canceled three or four times like this.

[GV3:P1660] I asked that mother, "Would you mind if I do something? I won't change your religion. And you don't need to change. Let me just take this." What was it? The items brought back from the festival were kept there. They agreed. I took these items and buried them near the entrance gate.

[GV3:P1661] I told them the marriage would take place within forty-one days. He is abroad. He has no time to wait anymore. It's a reasonably good job. Being in places like England, if you don't report on time, they will fire you. I told him to do one thing and left. The bride's family who refused due to his illness came back. The marriage took place within that time.

[GV3:P1662] What did you understand from this? We haven't traditionally assigned karma to women, have we? They believed in the Mother of Jesus. If this Mother hadn't given birth to Jesus, would she have been known? So many mothers have given birth. The greatness attributed to mothers who gave birth to great souls is due to the karma of giving birth to that Mahatma. What I am saying is, if you give agency to something that lacks the karma to fulfill a purpose, there will be some deficiencies. You may worship the Mother if you want. But it should be done by giving priority to Jesus.

[GV3:P1663] If Jesus didn't exist, would we know this Mother? Similar things exist in every philosophy. Muslims claim their faith is strict monotheism. However, there is no one else who believes in such blindness as they do. They are very afraid of Jinn. They worship Jinn.

[GV3:P1664] We cannot move forward without altering everything that is worshipped in this manner. It is the consequences of changing all this that I am suffering today. Won't at least some of that affliction come to me? It cannot be avoided without taking it on.

[GV3:P1665] We should not say that nothing that has come down through our tradition has value. Everything exists. The one who says it doesn't exist is not qualified to do anything. How should we transform what exists? That is our goal.

[GV3:P1666] Muslims call it the stream of light. The stream of light belongs to the seventh stage. We only see the sun, moon, and earth. This earth stands at a distance of nine such celestial bodies. As long as we stand on this earth and these seven bodies have a form, we can see their light. The other two remain like clouds of smoke. One can only say the divine presence has been attained after seeing all these seven bodies. We can give it any name we prefer—divine presence, presence of God, or presence of Brahman. We must be allowed passage corresponding to the merit of whichever name is used. He must have seen what blocks the place that grants such passage. He is removing that block. By removing it, he is granting the knowledge to see this light oneself. That depends on the firmness of his faith.

[GV3:P1667] This is what is said: You believe first. Other things come later. You shouldn't waste time investigating. First, believe. It is seen only in that faith. The one who sees like this is called a visionary. A visionary must see the heaven (dimension) corresponding to the level he stands in. We are not wishing for it; it is seen by itself. One who has seen like this can cure any obstacle or disease of yours. If there is blind faith, there is nothing in this world that cannot be resolved.

[GV3:P1668] Guruvani

[GV3:P1669] Santhigiri Janmadinapoojitha Samarppanappathippu - 2001

[GV3:P1670] To Bring Our Character to the Guru

[GV3:P1671] Consciousness has four states. Among them, even an ant has three—Jagrat (waking), Swapna (dreaming), and Sushupti (deep sleep). The fourth is Jagratsushupti (wakeful sleep). The knowledge attained in this state is Jnana (wisdom). Falsehood and worldly things will enter into this knowledge. The Guru exists to correct this falsehood. One's own karma trajectory will be seen in this. After this comes the absorptive state—a state where the external world is not known. If absorption continues seamlessly for three and three-quarter Nazhikas (about 90 minutes), Maha Samadhi occurs. Unless all this is experienced one by one, the truth will not be realized. Through heart-felt experience, by constantly seeing, one will merge into that light. After that, there is nothing.

[GV3:P1672] It is neither this nor that, not absolute meaning—

[GV3:P1673] Realizing clearly that it is immortal, being brave,

[GV3:P1674] Settling firmly that it is 'Sathom',

[GV3:P1675] One must settle softly, very softly.

[GV3:P1676] Everything that is known is Turiya consciousness. Vyasa and Vasistha have said the same thing. God gave us such a great light, yet we failed to respect and recognize it. You do not come here to know any of this. You come for diseases, women, and property. I will answer all of this. Why? So you can become like me.

[GV3:P1677] You do not know how to worship or pray. What a glorious thing God has given us! It is said that Sankaracharya gave it to Padmatirtha. Only the destined ones will get it. Jesus only had twelve disciples. Only two or three are known. All of them came for this. They didn't abandon it just because they didn't get it immediately.

[GV3:P1678] When you come here, peace of mind and healing of diseases are at the forefront. Everything can be achieved through this—if you are willing to suffer. If you don't achieve it through suffering, that achievement won't be respected. The three Gunas (qualities) exist in all human beings. Understand this much patiently. Everything else is like luck. When the duties of household life (Grihasthashrama dharma) are completed, renunciation (Sanyasa) will fall into their hands.

[GV3:P1679] Those who stand here should love whoever they see. It should be an imperishable love. It shouldn't be a hugging kind of love. You shouldn't love by giving wealth either. This is a new path. The love should be suited to this path. With love, honesty, and humility, you can achieve everything.

[GV3:P1680] As far as we are concerned, this is an aspect of compassion. A perfectly honest path. We do not need anyone else's method. There might be some kind of falsehood in all those. The nature of this ashram did not arise from anyone's cleverness among us. It was received as a divine concept corresponding to the greatness of the times.

[GV3:P1681] This is Kali Yuga. This time is perfectly good to fully understand the merit of goodness. It is said in the Bhagavatam that women will roam around as fake ascetics in Kali Yuga. It is seen true today. Today there is a place for hypocrisy, and a place for truth. In the past, goodness would not be opposed. Today, even if goodness is opposed, it can be spread. This is a quality of Kali Yuga.

[GV3:P1682] A novelty of this ashram is that even householders are able to see and experience that this is true. This is the other side of fake asceticism. This is absolute goodness. But women have an impurity. If they dislike someone, they have a tendency to destroy that person. It is also difficult for them to have pentad-purity. They will forget anyone and cause sorrow for small things. They will abandon their father, son, husband, or anyone for small matters. They should have the thought of how to seek refuge from this flaw.

[GV3:P1683] I had to hear bad words because I let women stay here. If at least one woman had thought about it, they would never have gone into alien behavior. The women who come here and the women who stay here should think about how to attain this good fortune received by divine will. This opportunity is for the growth, elevation, and perfection of their lineage. They should acquire this with the thought that they will not lose it.

[GV3:P1684] History shows that all great souls have hesitated to keep womankind close. But we must interact with them affectionately and convince them of this. Women also have this duty. Instead of showing that duty, they will either quarrel with those who speak ill or remain silent. It is not like that, if abused, it means we have won. You should acquire the karmic ability to approach and interact with those who speak ill. It should be there not only for a woman, but also for a man. It is when the course of karma shakes and settles down that one should set out to understand them. Otherwise, they will not care if spoken to.

[GV3:P1685] When a few things are done in devotion, some people start to feel hatred. Some others start to feel love. Through this, a part of karmic flaws goes away and another part increases. It will keep repeating like this. Thus, after right and wrong coming many times, it will finally become right. A little time is enough to cause bad things. Doing good things will cause double harm, making even a small mistake turn bad. While bad easily spreads, good works only like chaff catching fire.

[GV3:P1686] Those who stay here should have mutual love and understanding. It is not love by hugging and holding hands. Even if saying one word, it should be given a greatness. Giving that greatness, mutually respect each other. If we have a flaw, we should not let others understand it. Those who come here should not dare to correct those who stay here. But they can correct with love or respect. Due to meritorious elements or fortune levels, even if householders say it, ascetics and brahmacharins should obey. How to understand that this householder is elevated by meritorious elements and fortune levels? When seeing a person, the ascetic and brahmacharin should understand what kind of person they are. Thus, a brahmacharin can observe and learn the various behaviors of those who come here.

[GV3:P1687] When starting ashram life, what attention, vigilance, and virtues should we have? The mischief of a man or a woman is enough to destroy what we are struggling to build up. Such things should be guarded very strongly. Moreover, our state of mind from the morning, the good and bad arising from it, all such details should be written down. How many people spoke what, what they did, all this should be written and kept by those who wish to stay here till the end. When it is like this, we can understand everything and avoid some of the flaws happening to the ashram.

[GV3:P1688] No matter how lowly one is, if they stay here for a month, that much change will come. It is the householders who should change them, not the ascetics. Householders should never waste the opportunity they get for it. The karma done to benefit the world, as informed by the Guru, should never go wrong. Only then can greatness be brought to the ashram. Any person can rise through the thought that the Guru's word should not be broken. Doing any karma thinking it is for the world and offering it to God will yield the result of living many lifetimes. Due to that, our karmic course will pass away and the meritorious element will increase.

[GV3:P1689] Whatever you do here will first spread here. Then it will spread in the world. Devotion without attention has no place, and there is no use for that devotion. Those who stay in the ashram should stand up to the dignity of the ashram. Acting purely here, that should be spread to the world. If a matter happening here is not respected, it will be a loss for the world. If one unnecessary thing is said from here, it will result in two.

[GV3:P1690] Do not take any matter lightly. It will destroy you somewhere. Then even I will not be able to do anything. Things happening here are by divine will. You should finish your part as soon as possible. He who has done anything for the prosperity of the ashram, that prosperity will come into his life too. He who does without looking for anything will receive everything. Those destined to perish will not have immediate results. If someone is seen with a dark face, he should be sent away immediately. Otherwise, staying will cause harm. Whoever it is, only karmas conducive to spreading greatness should be done here.

[GV3:P1691] Guru Vani

[GV3:P1692] Santhigiri Birthday Poojitha Dedication Edition - 2001

[GV3:P1693] Good and Evil on the Path of Culture

[GV3:P1694] There is no other way for belief than blind belief. Whether we speak of rationalism or liberation, we can only act on what we know. We can only talk beyond that. People will say many things like that.

[GV3:P1695] One familiarity among Hindus is the Trimurti tradition. The Trimurti tradition has been around for a long time. It is mostly practiced by Brahmins. Nobody will do anything to ruin themselves voluntarily. When it comes through long familiarity, what is wrong seems right to us.

[GV3:P1696] Regarding culture, it will not go beyond a limit of knowledge. We each get only the knowledge useful for life. Any power and intellect can be used. With science, one can go, come, and stand in many places. Because of this, it cannot be said that we have understood the evolutionary nature of our life. We do not even know what evolution is.

[GV3:P1697] Life exists. And it evolves. Many thinking habits have come to us, and we practice and speak of each at different times, carrying it along as home, land, and country. We have been celebrating this for so long! But no big change has happened because of that. There is a reason for it. What is the use of saying it is bad or good without seeing it? We can say whatever we want, but is there any practical path?

[GV3:P1698] Through which path have we become bad? It is from our ancestors, or from the Vedic scholars, or from our Guru's guidance. Not from other fields. This is a fault within ourselves. So there is no point in blaming anyone. We also did not try to end this fault. Wife, husband, children, wealth, lordship, education - we are all swaying these, and from that sway we are constantly increasing ideas in a way that we can never recover. We do all this because we have bodily comfort and life inside it. If that comfort disappears for a moment, all this swaying ends.

[GV3:P1699] How can we end this sway? Will it end by being satisfied with the logic of some miraculous feats and Vedantic practices that say, holding God will end it, holding it this way will end it, holding it that way will end it? Believing in so many miraculous feats, blindly believing, that blindness has wreaked havoc on us. Not just common people. There is no use in being called a scholar or a perfect sage. What do these ordinary sages know, other than finishing today what was seen yesterday? It is just like telling a local story: during my uncle's time, not even a fly would go through here; now an elephant can pass, what a pity! What greatness is there in this other than such talk? I don't believe, I believe, these are all nonsense, good things, bad things - if we finish with so many rationalizations, will it be an effort to change this? Who is smart in this? Everyone is smart indeed.
