[GV3:P2260] Guru Vani

[GV3:P2261] Santhigiri Birthday Poojitha Dedication Edition - 2001

[GV3:P2262] Sweet Memories in the Path of Remembrance (From Guru's Memories)

[GV3:P2263] One will only see the procession of dharma and karma that solidified through their gloriously sacrificial lives in their original form in the lives of the great souls not only of Hindus, but also of Islam, Christian, Jain lineages and all other lineages of sacrifice. There is no history of contemporaries ever respecting and knowing these sacrificial lives. In that great sacrifice where the life from birth was completely shared for the people, the part played by a few poor householders who did not have much intellectual ability or education to speak of, stands as a mirror of memory filling the whole world. In the lives of all great souls, all the dharmas and karmas meant for these poor blessed householders have been endorsed similarly to the model of sacrifice where Krishna told Arjuna 'Mamekam Saranam Vraja' (Take refuge in Me alone). In my Guru's life too, the only asset was a group of ignorant disciples, including me.

[GV3:P2264] Many have shared my Guru's sacrifice. But the sacrifice of one or two families will never fade from the path of memory. Very few people do what role a householder should play in the life of a renunciate with complete humility and honesty, according to their moral code. Those who were with me and helped me during my period of Avadhoota (asceticism) were the owners of a sense of dharma that reminded one of the dharma of Kuchela.

[GV3:P2265] The life of Muthappan and his wife, who lived in a street in Pettah paying a rent of six rupees and earned their livelihood with a bullock cart, was a perfect example of the reality of life. As a disciple of the Guru and his wife, the devotion and respect they showed to other disciples of the Guru attained complete love for the Guru and was such that their adherence to dharma never left them. This husband and wife were owners of sacrifice with a full heart to know and fulfill whatever needs the Guru and his disciples had. They also had four children.

[GV3:P2266] I still remember certain acts the Guru used to show towards them. The strict adherence to dharma that the Guru practiced with them, even while the hunger of those six stomachs persisted, is something that will never fade from the memories of life. Moreover, when I think that the wealth of disciples who had to participate in the lives of great souls, who truly deserve to be called renunciates by the world, was confined to these two people in my Guru's life, it becomes cool, loving tributes with a full heart. I think the people of the world must definitely understand this.

[GV3:P2267] In those days, when I used to go to see the Guru - whenever I went - besides bringing whatever the Guru needed to eat, they also strictly observed the dharma of providing food for those who accompanied him. They were workers who stood highest in the principle of 'Sankalpa Karma Manasa' (intention, action, mind) and the foremost followers of dharma in sacrifice. Three of Muthappan's children were studying in school. The fourth was an infant. This bullock cart driver fulfilled all the needs of the Guru and the other disciples with the Guru, as well as the needs of their children! They slept on pieces of sackcloth, making a screen with sacks on the veranda and courtyard of the six-rupee rented house. Only memories of brave karma warriors are capable of giving such goosebumps.

[GV3:P2268] Those children were incapable of recognizing anything. Yet, the reality that those children, who had the mind to give anything, experienced and lived to know cannot be forgotten by anyone. From among the disciples who were praying with their eyes closed, the Guru called both of them and said in a language mixed with Tamil, "I have to go to Guru Samadhi this evening. I don't have money for the travel fare." Saying "I will do as ordered immediately", he got up, went and sold the bullock cart, and brought the cash. From the next day, he bought goods from merchants in Chalai and Pettah and carried them as headload. Then he could be seen as someone serving his master with utmost sincerity, involved in the work his master liked most.

[GV3:P2269] Those were not days like today when there are labor issues, conflicts between employers and employees, or sabotages. It was a time when the worker happily received whatever the employer lovingly and knowingly gave. Let me point out one thing that came to my mind about that situation back then. I have felt that the Guru had a mind without any compassion. On that day when he said he had no money to go to the Guru's Samadhi place, when the cash kept in the four sides of his waistband and tucked into his pockets was taken out and counted, there was six hundred and seventy-four rupees. By the standards of those days, this much money was enough for two people to go to the Samadhi place in Thanjavur and return. I could only think that while having this much money in hand, hurting this poor disciple with a word saying "I have no money in hand" and making him sell even his bullock cart to raise cash, was a cruel act. Or if Muthappan was a little late to come, the Guru, along with the accompanying disciples, would have gone to his house sometime after dusk, eaten whatever was available—be it tapioca, black tea, or rice water—and slept spreading the sacks. Witnessing that hurried task of going to some shop next morning to fetch breakfast for the Guru and his disciples, I would often secretly wipe my tears. I have often wondered why a householder, an owner of a life with nothing, who had no mentionable name or fame, and had not earned anyone's respect in the land, was making so much sacrifice for a man and his disciples.

[GV3:P2270] One day the Guru told me, "You can go after you gain the experience." Then the Guru called this same man, took money from him, and took me along to Thuckalay. Going to a mango tree in the compound of the Thuckalay Munsiff Court, he summoned Parameswaran Pillai, a process server at the Munsiff Court. He came running from a nearby shop. He told Parameswaran Pillai to call Meeran Pillai, a disciple there, and eight others. They belonged to different castes and religions. As per the Guru's instructions, all of them were brought before nine o'clock at night. Seating all of them, he praised me. Then he said, "From tomorrow onwards, everyone should come and sleep here at night." On the third day at midnight, I informed the Guru about the ignorance of my mind on that day. It was nothing else but about taking money from this Parameswaran Pillai and Muthappan by causing them so much sorrow. Suddenly, with anger, he called out "Eda" (Hey). Then he said, "There is no need to see whether they are rich or beggars. Making them do it by giving them sorrow is to bring them some good. Most of these householders come to achieve their selfish ends. Otherwise, they are not people with any aspect of sacrifice. Why did you think they are like you? You are someone who has experienced sorrows and endured sacrifices by running and making others run an organization. They do not have such an experience. Among many such people, only one or two have the mind to do any sacrifice. Before coming to me, Parameswaran Pillai had sold all his property, which would have been worth two and a half lakh rupees, drank toddy, and ruined himself. Though Muthappan was not a drunkard like that, he had many other faults. Only if some sorrow like this comes, will the stain in their lives be cleared, and it will bring some good at least in their children's lives. Before coming to me, their lives were very bad. The fortunate one must have done all this."

[GV3:P2271] Although Swami is not one who gives importance to rebirths, he admits that there are rebirths. He told me that day, "According to the systems of Hindus and Islam, only if one crosses births will a visit be of use to a householder. Therefore, washing away their stains is the karma I am showing. If you don't understand this, you will understand shortly." Saying this much, the Guru sat quietly.

[GV3:P2272] The next day at three in the morning, some invisible power fell into my body. I felt I suddenly curled up like a ball. As if every hair follicle was being plucked out. I felt my body shattered like mustard seeds. Then the Guru struck me hard and asked, "Are you afraid and running away?" I said, "My mind is not afraid, but my body has collapsed." "Ah, it's okay, there is salvation," the Guru said. Following this, he also told Parameswaran Pillai to take leave. I have memory and thought. But I don't know what to do. If a vehicle comes opposite on the road, I don't know how to step aside. I didn't know why Parameswaran Pillai was asked to take leave.

[GV3:P2273] We three would walk. Swami would stand here and there and tell some stories. Sometimes it would take up to an hour and a half to walk a mile. Going to every temple, he would ask me, "Look there, what do you see?" He would ask the same to Parameswaran Pillai as well.

[GV3:P2274] On the eleventh day, we were taken to the Samadhi place of Peerukannu Sahib. Right at the doorway, my legs started to feel numb. While sitting there for ten to fifteen minutes, I saw lakhs of demons and gods lined up. I saw many jinns. After seeing all this, I said I wanted to see Swami. Pepper, salt, bananas, jaggery, etc., come there as offerings. While we were there, eight bunches of bananas arrived. Thus, we got eight bananas each. We ate them.

[GV3:P2275] When we returned that day, Swami was unusually laughing out loud. He talked about vision and the aspect of sight until half-past twelve. Guru said, "This is the work done to remove the calamity of a society. You must tell me when the visions of Chattampi Swami and Narayana Guru appear. You must tell me when you see the ancestors and worship deities of people from Vellala Chettis to Idiyans. I have to tell you some things." Half the time, he was praising me. Others were not enjoying it either.

[GV3:P2276] On the twenty-first day, Swami told a story. The story was about the Samadhi on top of the Kattuvasahib hill. He asked Meeran Pillai from Thuckalay, "Do you know the greatness of Peerukannu Sahib?" He replied he didn't know. Peerukannu Sahib was someone who walked along with a Hindu Sanyasi named Kattuvasahib. They both sat on that hill. Kattuvasahib attained Samadhi there. The road is at least two miles away from this rock. There is no other way. It cannot be carried away either. A big man. Peerukannu Sahib collected the round stones available there and piled them over the body. That took three days. It was to protect the body from foxes. This greatness is what is seen in Peerukannu Sahib.

[GV3:P2277] Thus, after eleven days, one night he started praising me again. Those who didn't like this wouldn't come along. It was decided to go to Kattuvasahib hill carrying water and bananas. Swami, myself, and five others climbed the hill. We went there and sat under a banyan tree. Then Parameswaran Pillai stood up and said that many demons, gods, and sanyasis in subtle forms were passing by. I could not see any of that. I only saw a light filling the entire Samadhi place like smoke. Everyone else with us said they saw many things at various places. I got nothing.

[GV3:P2278] When it reached this point, Swami suddenly got a rush to leave. Though exhausted and tired, everyone walked quickly. My only possessions, walking for days without washing or bathing, were a soiled and stinking dhoti, a shirt, and an umbrella. Swami snatched the shirt, dhoti, and umbrella from me. He took a towel from his bag, which looked like it belonged to a cabaret dancer, gave it to me, and asked me to wear it. I wore it. Saying "Run Eda," he gave me a slap. Afraid of where to go, I ran. I looked back only after running a furlong.

[GV3:P2279] The entire expense of those who were with us that day was borne by Parameswaran Pillai. His total salary was forty-five rupees. The credit at the shop would be at least seven hundred rupees. At home, his wife lived wrapping just a piece of a sari to cover her honor.

[GV3:P2280] Thus, this Parameswaran Pillai and Muthappan were the helpers of Swami. Two sacrificial families who lived maintaining the disciple's dharma within the householder's dharma. How have they imbibed the greatness of the Guru-disciple relationship? How have they acted? Let me offer my respectful salutations before the blessedness shown by these families who had no education, wealth, or anything mentionable.

[GV3:P2281] After Swami's Samadhi, Muthappan bought land, built a house, and got his children married. Everyone got life's comforts. One of them is a policeman, one owns a lorry. He is also a headload workers' leader.

[GV3:P2282] During my Avadhoota period, looking at the magnitude of my Guru's sacrifice, I did not even know what sacrifice was. Let me mention some aspects of the Guru's sacrifice here. He hadn't reduced the duties according to Islamic law one bit. He had also taught alternately in four Arabic schools. At the age of twenty-seven, his wife and children died suddenly. For an individual who is a believer and a devotee, this is a favorable circumstance to suddenly jump into asceticism, isn't it?

[GV3:P2283] The Guru had already understood from books about the places to catch hold of awakened Samadhis. He had also thought about a suitable place for it. Moreover, there was a Samadhi place of a Guru in Thanjavur that he had imagined in his mind till then. Apart from this Samadhi place, he has also talked to me about three or four other mosques. One of them is the mosque known as Nagore in Valapattanam. Today that place has been enclosed. Which of these should he accept as his Guru?

[GV3:P2284] The Channankara stream flows south, reaches Ambalathara, and then goes west, right? There is a bridge in between, before reaching Poonthura, which is further west from there. Around that bridge, there is a place intertwined with forest, open space, and bushes. This place is known as Samadhikkara. If one travels barely two miles from Beemapally southwards on the road passing through Samadhikkara, one can reach Poonthura. This is a coastal road. No one except fishermen lived on the sides of the road.

[GV3:P2285] West of Pettah is Chacka. South of Chacka is this open space. The calls to prayer (Azan) from Beemapally and Vallakkadavu mosque can be heard if one sits here. The Guru usually sits in this place. Not many people walk this way. People were afraid to walk south along the banks of the stream. Women never traveled that way. There are reasons for that. This was a place where they dumped the dead bodies of those killed in violence, which was very common in every nook and corner of the town then. This place was also the habitat of a breed of dogs with unusually long tails, known in the old days as 'Sengottai dogs'. One would get scared just seeing those dogs. If they saw a person alone, the dog would run near, raise both its front legs, place them on the person's body, and bite randomly. That person would die of fear falling there. Once dead, the dog would drag the body away. The Guru used to perform penance sitting in the bushes in that place.

[GV3:P2286] He would do penance there for forty-one days without any food. Even for passing urine and stool, he would only shift places within that bush. Except for shifting here and there from where he sits, he wouldn't get up from there. After a week, there wouldn't even be any passing of urine and stool. Then there is no need to shift either. When he sits like this for forty-one days without food and water, the body first dries up like dried ginger, and then, because there is no blood left at all, water starts accumulating in the body. Just as objects are reflected in a well-polished and kept kindi (pitcher) or jug, one can see the reflection of a person passing by far away in this body. After another forty-one days, this body will dry up again and become a shriveled object.

[GV3:P2287] He discovered that any kind of Samadhi can be awakened through this kind of penance. Over six years, he awakened the Samadhi by performing three such penances and discovered in which paths the aspect of vision lies. This is the method the Guru adopted to acquire wisdom.

[GV3:P2288] After this, he happened to get a book the size of a matchbox. It is recorded in that book what kind of place one should catch hold of the awakened Samadhi. He has shown me that book. It is recorded in that book that if one awakens the Samadhis at Nagore mosque, another mosque in Thanjavur, a Samadhi there itself, and the Samadhi at Beemapally, one can attain such and such states. It was also discovered that these places have certain peculiarities. The road from Kanyakumari along the coast and otherwise towards the northwest is included in the equator. It is based on this that the Gandhi Memorial is established in Kanyakumari. On one day of the year, sunlight will strike the Samadhi through the glass on top of the memorial. (The equator passes through parts of Singapore as well.) The equator passes northwards through Valapattanam and Nagore too. The spread of the equator's aura, which separates the northern and southern parts of the earth, can be felt up to three hundred miles wide in some places. It is in these zones of influence that Samadhi places and mosques are established. Scientists have established the Gandhi Memorial taking this into account. It is believed that these places have a special spiritual aura. As one crosses the Arabian deserts and goes northwest, this aura gradually decreases.

[GV3:P2289] Avadhootas walk looking for places with more spiritual aura. In places where the sun's aura falls more, the heat will be higher. Avadhootas and their Samadhis are more in such places. On many days, the Guru has read and explained to me what the previously mentioned book has recorded about this subject. While sleeping on the beach, he would wake me up at two in the morning saying the next auspicious time (Brahmamuhurtham according to Hindu justice) is arriving, untie and open the fishermen's net-keeping hut, pile sand around so the lamp won't blow out in the wind, light a big candle, and read this Urdu book, giving the meaning and explaining it to me. Sleeping is also inside that.

[GV3:P2290] According to this book, the reason for the increase in the residence of Avadhootas in Samadhi places is considering the qualities of the sun's aura. Among the Guru's disciples, he has taught such a secret only to me. Other than telling the rest that there is such knowledge in the book, he hasn't shown this book to anyone. Needless to say, when compared to such a great sacrifice of the Guru, I hadn't even known what sacrifice was in my life till then.

[GV3:P2291] Avadhootas walk and walk and sometimes die without reaching the goal. There are many such people who haven't reached the spiritual fortune of attaining the goal. Some only reach up to a small part of the Samadhi. Others might reach up to a quarter. There are also those who complete it over two or three lifetimes. In a person's life, awakening a Samadhi doesn't happen more than three times at the most. The duration of awakening one Samadhi is eighteen and a half years. Even for those who have acquired merit by birth, it takes a minimum of seven and a half years.

[GV3:P2292] The verse in the Gita 'Pratyakshavaham Dharmam' prophesies that Samadhi can be awakened through various kinds of vision aspects too. Anyway, only an individual who has awakened at least three Samadhis in this manner can complete the part called 'message' meant for that. These are all things my Guru told me on days when no other disciples were around, during the time between half-past twelve at night and six in the morning. Certain hidden aspects remain concealed in the process of awakening Samadhi. None of that can be revealed here.

[GV3:P2293] What I am narrating here as sweet memories in the path of remembrance is with the desire that if this reality of life, solidified in the excellence of compassion and the depth of love, can be made at least like the merit of life for a few, let it be so.

[GV3:P2294] What usually happens is that Avadhootas die somewhere even when they reach a thousandth part of the sun's aura on the equator. There are also Samadhi places of those who die when they reach the second, third, fourth, and fifth of these states among crores of such people. Out of that, from top to bottom up to the third state, troubles from people will be less. The huge group of souls who died wandering as devotees completing from the first to the second state are seen reflected in the spiritual form of the lives of people who start reaching the state of gods, completing two and a half. Passing the fifth state and crossing five and a half, six, and up to seven, the visionary insight called Bhumika will end. By the time these states are completed, the Bhumika up to the stellar system will also be finished. When they say that out of lakhs of people with such vision, only one or two become true visionaries, they mean such people.

[GV3:P2295] The souls who died after attaining the second state, if they see an Avadhoota who is gaining in the aspect of vision, give him vision, show and make him show wondrous sights, and free from honor and dishonor before the public, they are the ones who died distributing a great treasury of wondrous feats before the people, making them think he is one. What Asan has said as "The spiritual yogi who walks like winds, like growing infants, like a demon, like an absolute fool, without illusions, devoid of intellect, devoid of deception" is about such yogis who show highly wondrous arrays of siddhis. People mostly know only about such people. These are the ones who have lived for seven or eight hundred years through some kalpa-seva (rejuvenation therapy), walked around showing tricks, and fell into a trap. Those who are not skilled in kalpa-seva walk as ascetics, show the above-mentioned wonders, and act like those who are beyond all rules.

[GV3:P2296] If you walk from Kanyakumari this way in South Travancore, you can see people narrating many stories about such yogis giving them an aura of wonder. The exaggerated depiction that the person known as Kompaswami attained Samadhi at seven places is an example of this. They say that after he finally attained Samadhi at Thengapattanam, no one has seen him. Another person depicted in this manner is a woman named Mayiamma who walked in the ascetic state. By recording that when Sree Narayana Guru laid under the Suchindram chariot, he saw her limbs catching fire, this woman too became an owner of history. There is also a story that she gave him a mango. There is a woman like that even now in Kanyakumari who lives without covering her nakedness and in many ways. I have heard her too being called Mayiamma. A rich man has even built a mandapam on the north side of the road going west from Kanyakumari in the name of this Mayiamma.

[GV3:P2297] There are countless stories depicted like this. Kudil Swami, Vatti Swami, Chatti Swami, Chunnambu Swami, Mannenna Swami, Kesavan Sanyasi, Ayya Swami in Trivandrum area, Sambrani Swami, Samadhithoppu, Manacaud Samadhi place, Kalladi Masthan, Kaniyapuram Thangal - I know the stories of many such people depicted by the people and experienced by me.

[GV3:P2298] Many stories are also told about another superior type of people who have gone through kalpa-seva, and about Pakkanar, Naranath Bhranthan who belonged to the Parayi Petta Panthirukulam, and about Thiruvalluvar. In addition, Subramanya, his Guru Bhogar the wise, and Hidumban all belong to the lineage of wondrous characters. There are many people all over Tamil Nadu who narrate such wondrous stories. The devotees of Subramanya form a big lineup of events scattering wonders today, piercing various types of spears, walking on thorn shoes, and dancing tying kavadi of fire, milk kavadi, fish kavadi, garuda kavadi, etc. People see all this as great too.

[GV3:P2299] Narayana Guru and Chattampi Swami have also shown siddhis for at least a short period. There are also disciples who have shown some tricks taking higher gurus as models. Thrissur Pambu Swami, Shubhanandan, and Swayamprakashini belong to this group. Before the woman now known as Vallikavilamma, there was a woman there who used to speak in a trance. One could see some swamis who had stayed near them for two or three years narrating many stories and getting amazed.

[GV3:P2300] Oachira history is no different from this either. One can see from the Oachira padanilam that a group of masseurs known as Oachira Vaidyans established a mutt there and placed the statue of Kabir Swami in it. It seems that mutt is now hidden by buildings. Thus, there is a history all over India of people with slight visionary insight getting trapped in wondrous tricks composed by scholars and walking around desiring wondrous siddhis and wondrous visions. It was mentioned earlier that some have said that crores of souls can be seen standing nowhere due to the above-mentioned aspect of vision. There is also no shortage of Vedanta scholars who have composed books praising them as visionaries, giving huge importance to sleight of hand tricks, siddhi paths, and Pranayama (Hatha Yoga) siddhis.

[GV3:P2301] There are writers and even scholars among us who become enthralled by wonders if told of each thing that generates an illusion of wonder, like 'like sunlight reflecting on a covered golden vessel' in the Isavasya Upanishad; and as said in a part of the Mandukya Upanishad that 'if a large bundle of torches is lit and swung vigorously in a circle at night from a distance beyond sight, it will generate the illusion of a fire pit'. It is through such writers and scholars that stories matching the dominance of the Devadasi system, which existed till recently, were spun, and girls were brought to the path of action making them maids of the deity. Places called Koothambalams that exist with such stories, dance, and music are still there today. Koothuparamba in Thalassery is one such place.

[GV3:P2302] This much is noted so that all God-fearing people may know. People who set out desiring to become knowers of the self often fall into the vision of souls tangled like this and wander aimlessly. Therefore, I apologize to the people living thirsty for the soul for the reason that I am late to inform about the memories in my path of remembrance. Along with that, I use this opportunity to remind you, who step out to respect the greatness of obligation to God, about the mistakes that can happen to you. These special blunders reach up to the story that says Padmapadar came to save Sankaracharya, who is praised as the world teacher, from the Kapalika who set out to cut his neck. Like ordinary people struggling after falling into some bottomless pit, to prevent you from getting bewildered by the mirage causing a loss of sense of time and space, and to prevent you from stumbling by falling into the misguided travels mentioned in this memory, I wish you well in the name of God, reminding the faithful servants of God once more.

[GV3:P2303] Guru Vani

[GV3:P2304] September 2001

[GV3:P2305] Those who practice yoga techniques like Hatha Yoga and Chandrika Yoga can show many wondrous powers. If one is a true devotee, he will stand only for God's will. This person will turn into a wise man. This wise man will not reject anything. If it is something that cannot be absorbed, he will seek a way to reach the goal while maintaining equanimity. This wise man will not show any of the methods of going after wonders for you who come to catch God with offerings and vows.

[GV3:P2306] Those who show wonders are the ones who do not know what the Yuga Dharma is. They will only spin inside the collections of books that scholars and laymen have written up calling it Dharma Mimamsa. These Dharma Mimamsas are blunders that happened from the refined language. This blunder has been going on for twenty-five Chatur Yugas. Therefore, we have not received the part of knowledge called Yuga Dharma. What exists currently is Kali Yuga. Kali Yuga can be completed by chanting the name of God (Namajapa). This is seen noted in many places. But when the Shaktheya, Shaiva, and Vaishnava systems were completed, each system changed the Yuga Dharma into their own ways.

[GV3:P2307] There is a commentary of the sorcerer - Madan, Nadan, Nadesan. That is, the sorcerer completed the scholarship in that way, becoming as powerful as Nadesan! The karma part written up by the educated in this way became void. Because of that, it became easy to complete the array of siddhis. Thus, they changed God into siddhi, wonder, and sleight of hand, and started flowing it through their hands.

[GV3:P2308] Wherever the Sudra dharma belonging to the Varnasrama dharma exists, it has gone bad. You who have failed like that, having got your time, are not making any effort to improve through that time. I have always told you, this time (Kali) is yours. Do not waste it. If you waste it, things from which you can never escape will come upon you.

[GV3:P2309] I am speaking about a work that God made us do here. Even those who were partners in that karma have not known what its greatness is. You saw here the karma that was taken up to the stellar system by doing and making others do variations of states. After that, it reached the ninth stage. This was completed with the help of Sree Krishna. After completing that stage, Sree Krishna himself said, 'Now you catch hold of your Guru and do it'. So when we caught hold of the Guru and did it, it succeeded. This should be called the secret of secrets. One can ask, can't this karma be entrusted to God himself, the Krishna who helped complete these states by doing this much help for my soul?

[GV3:P2310] This is the secret of secrets. That is, the will of God. How much did you believe and apply that will of God? Each of you applied your intellect. From that point, you became bad. But because you are ignorant, God tolerated it. If you still handle this without understanding it, I cannot do anything. God has paved this path that God tells and conducts for everyone coming, going, and staying here. This is what you have to understand. God tells that it should be carried forward for seven generations. If carried forward like that, the karma of seven Manvantaras comes into this lineage. Do you really know how to carry it forward as God tells? Never. But we don't need to know anything. It is enough if you just believe and go. You don't need to think of anything else. Perhaps, if you think, you will fall.

[GV3:P2311] The subtle form of great ones like Jesus, Nabi, and thousands of Gurus who have come till today comes and stands before you and tells you that this should be preserved. To make you do their desire, people connected to that lineage will be brought here somehow. Whether they take it or not is another matter. But you are not taking it. How many thousands have gone unable to take it. Because there are many difficulties to open the door of many past lives that happened due to the birth of each one of you. Even after being told and shown that this life kept claiming this ideology is of each one of you, you do not understand.

[GV3:P2312] It is not necessary for all of you to understand the deep knowledge of this. But you must understand that this is the truth. When you are convinced that this must be completed somehow, you should not insert your own intellect into it. One might think, can't God's will be done by God himself, what do others have to do with it? God will not break the law. Even children who were fully convinced that God will not break the law have gone wrong. All of them are my contemporaries and known to you. But has anyone of you thought about why they went wrong? Perhaps, if you think, will you be convinced?

[GV3:P2313] That is why I said this is the most difficult subject. None of you should insert any of your intellect into this. That blessedness failed to ripen inside those who inserted their intellect like that. I cannot do anything here. I am just being kept as an instrument to show you. If someone behaves from within this without understanding it, their intellect will be slowly turned. Later the house will be turned. Ultimately, it will reach a character that turns the land. You should handle this with complete understanding.

[GV3:P2314] We are souls carrying a pile of mistakes. We cannot suddenly understand and correct it as soon as we see it. I cannot say that those who believe, do guru pooja, and obey here have understood even one-hundredth of this ideology. I am talking about those who live saying it is for this. There is nothing to say about others. Mistakes have been repeating for many ages. Whoever has come to correct that mistake has only been destroyed. The biggest proof of that is Sree Krishna. Everyone who came till today after Sree Krishna. You are not people who do not know this. Because it was shown to you. You must preserve what you have seen without losing it. If you lose it, I cannot do anything.

[GV3:P2315] The Akhandanamam (continuous chanting) here was given at Varkala. But there was no way to do it there. There was no one with the suitable knowledge there. When I came to Pothencode again, I was told to do it. I did. You know that we were saved only through the Akhandanamam here. You should not take this as my name. In Kali, Namajapam is said for salvation. This is a completely new name created for that. It was created for that, making me merely an instrument.

[GV3:P2316] There is a demonic dharma standing as karma all over this atmosphere. It is purely a demonic dharma. If you or I try to chase it, it won't run away. If this name is chanted, all this demonic dharma will move away. This demonic dharma has to be removed for the sake of our children. A traditional knowledge has been kept for us as birth and death. In that, the mistake caused by birth should leave us. The good should be preserved. They will make us do a karma using that good at the end.

[GV3:P2317] You saw so much bad. You saw so much good. Containing all that in the light of the Guru, destroying and purifying it as if by lightning. If you cannot do that karma now, you won't be able to be saved even in a thousand births. With that, you are destroying your own blessed karma. You decide what you want next. This mistake should not come through your children. If it comes, it is not something that can be resolved by going and apologizing to anyone. It is not possible even if you pledge your life.
